# Roadmap

The goal, stated as a capability target: **every capability a mature,
widely deployed PDF engine offers, every capability the rest of the field
offers, and more — and every document a real producer emits opens, renders
provably the way the world renders it, round-trips, signs, conforms and is
searchable, on every target, deterministically, with no unsafe code, no
third-party logic and no silent failure, at a speed with a number behind
it.** The feature docs record what already holds; this file lists what does
not yet, and nothing else. What has left it is in git history and in the
design docs' own "as built" sections, not here.

**What is in scope is settled; what comes first is measured.** On
9 October 2026 the owner decided that this engine has every capability the
field offers, and more: speed verified by tests and the engine optimised
against them; form JavaScript as a complete implementation; signing with a
private key in the engine; OCR in scope and last, as a later
implementation; and everything else such an engine offers. Every row in
this file is in scope and none waits for a corpus to ask for it. Ruling 3
is amended, not retired ([rulings.md](rulings.md)): corpus hit-rate
evidence no longer decides *whether* a capability is built, it decides *the
order* inside a tier — the row a real document needs goes before the row
none has asked for yet. The tiers run in order: 0 (measure), 1
(correctness), P (performance), the capability tiers by area, container
depth, and OCR, which is last by the owner's instruction. Every manual
verification and every decision that needs a person is in the last
section, "Human verification and owner decisions", and nowhere else; a row
whose only remainder is a person's act moves there.

Nothing in this file names another engine, viewer, validator or document
tool — the corpora included, each of which is named by its role. A
capability is described on its own terms; "the field" means what a reader
of PDFs is entitled to expect. Where a number was set by a planning-time
comparison, the comparison was run once, is a dated measurement kept
outside this repository, and is never re-run against a build (H-112); the
number stands on its own terms here and its exit criterion is first-party.

Every item carries its **evidence** (a measured number, a named refusal, or
"absent — the owner's parity decision of 9 October 2026" where nothing in
the tree measures the gap, with the search or the line that establishes
it), its **exit criterion** (a first-party test or
CI job that runs — an item without one is not done when the code lands, it
is done when the check goes green) and a **size band**: S ≈ 0.5
engine-months, M ≈ 1–2, L ≈ 2–4, XL ≈ 5–8, and nothing larger — a bigger
piece of work is split into milestones, each its own row. L and XL items
have a design doc in [design/](design/), written before the item is
scheduled; the **Doc** column links it where it exists and marks it *owed*
where it does not. **Depends on** names the rows, and the person's acts in
the last section (the H rows), an item waits for. Every number and every
`file:line` pointer below was read from the tree on 9 October 2026, the
day this file was replanned, or carries its own date; a number this file
carries and the tree contradicts is a defect in this file.

The measurements the tiers are ordered by, from `corpus/ratchet.json` and
its two font-bearing siblings:

| Measure | Value |
| --- | ---: |
| Corpus files (a viewer's test suite 974, an archival validator's corpus 2 907, a structural tool's suite 637, an association's examples 7, a web crawl 1 000) | 5 525 |
| Render every page | 5 516 |
| Do not (6 in the structural tool's suite, 3 in the web crawl; every one a file that would not open) | 9 |
| Rendered with something reported, no faces / synthetic face / bundled faces | 1 412 / 459 / 495 |
| `rotate` held of asked | 4 998 of 5 114 |
| `rotate-tight` held of asked | 4 583 of 5 114 |
| `crop` held of asked | 4 929 of 5 075 |
| `dpi` held of asked | 5 342 of 5 457 |

The suite stands at 6 819 passed, 0 failed, 63 ignored across 289 suites as
[verification.md](verification.md) records it, measured 9 October 2026 on
`x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`.

## How this file changes

An item leaves this file when its exit criterion is green, and its feature
doc's refusal table loses the matching row in the same commit. There is one
other way out and it is not a shortcut: an item whose exit criterion can
never go green, for a reason outside this repository's power, is not work
this file is tracking, it is a **limit**, and it moves to Named non-goals
with the reason it cannot close. Design docs in [design/](design/) carry
scope, non-goals, design, milestones with concrete exit criteria, and
risks, in that order.

- **An exit criterion is a first-party test or CI job** (ruling 13). An
  outside program may host the code, execute it, fetch bytes for it or
  generate a fixture once; it never says whether an output is right. A
  planning-time comparison may set a target number and nothing else, and
  it is never named here.
- **Every new capability brings its speed row with it** into the benchmark
  set (MS-04) and its determinism fingerprint (K-07) in the commit that
  closes it.
- **A row whose only remainder is a person's act moves to the last
  section**, leaving its machine half (if any) in its tier.
- **Named non-goals hold hard limits only** — a reason outside this
  repository's power. A scope choice is a row, however late its tier.
- **Rows enter with evidence**: a named refusal, a test, a corpus number,
  or "absent — the owner's parity decision of 9 October 2026" where nothing
  in the tree measures the gap, followed by how the absence was
  established — the search that finds nothing, quoted so it can be re-run,
  or the line or refusal that says so.
- **Bands and floors move one way.** Lowering a ratchet floor or widening a
  benchmark band is a reviewed act (H-78).

## What "best" means here

The target is not a list of features but a set of axes, each with a number
that can only move one way. A capability the field offers and this engine
lacks is a row in a capability tier. An axis nobody measures is a row in
tier 0, and it comes first, because a claim with no ratchet behind it is
the kind this repository has already caught itself making.

| Axis | Measured today | What gives it a ratchet |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Correctness on documents nobody here wrote | 5 525 files in five pinned corpora — a viewer's test suite, an archival validator's conformance corpus, a structural tool's suite, an association's examples, and **1 000 documents a crawler found on the open web** spanning 462 distinct `/Producer` strings; 5 516 render every page | `corpus/ratchet.json`, nightly, every failure attributed by producer — **but the nightly fails on the stale default branch every night and has measured nothing of the October work** (MS-11, MS-13) |
| **Speed** | seven criterion operations on 3-page inputs, weekly, on the default branch only; `crates/tinker-pdf/benches/baseline.json` records each one's fastest figure on `ubuntu-latest` over fifteen dispatches of one revision, with bands of 35 % to 135 % over a two-cluster runner fleet; "render text at 150 dpi" draws no glyphs | **counted-work guards in `cargo test` on every pull request and target (MS-01); a paired wall-clock gate on every pull request against the merge base and against the planning baseline, which holds every tier-P target as a factor, a calibration or a relation with no person in the loop (MS-02); an absolute ratchet on a pinned reference runner (MS-03)** |
| Memory | 65 caps in `bounds_ledger.rs`, two of them the runtime bounds a *process* spends and one a relation between a symbol and its page rather than a quantity; every corpus child measures its own peak resident set and `ratchet.json` bands the per-corpus maximum within a **measured 2 %** tolerance | per-operation peak bands (MS-05) |
| Footprint | **6.76 MB of wasm, 3.28 MB gzipped** (9 October 2026), **over** `release.yml`'s 2.5 MiB gzipped gate, which only the release checks | **a CI ratchet on every pull request, per build profile (MS-06)**; the budget is the owner's (H-04) |
| Determinism | 15 render fingerprints and 3 document hashes on linux, windows, macos and `wasm32-wasip1` | extended to every subsystem this file adds, parallel and cached paths included (K-07, MS-01's A9) |
| Fidelity | arithmetic fixtures, metamorphic relations, committed fingerprints; nine reviewed goldens never reviewed; ruling 13's amendment of 5 September 2026 on dated outside measurements | reviewed goldens (H-60) and the new families (K-08, H-73) |
| Conformance | PDF/A agreement 1 948 of 2 371 (measured before the October rules); PDF/UA 29 of 239 caught, 0 false alarms (before the facade); html5lib 1 779 of 1 784; CommonMark 651 of 652; UAX #14 19 338 of 19 338 | each suite's pass count ratcheted, and the ECMAScript conformance suite run to its end for the 2025 editions (FJ-01…FJ-03f); PKI path-validation data (SG-11), crypto vectors, the PDF object grammar (SD-20), PDF/A, UA and X (SD rows) |
| Robustness | 51 fuzz targets nightly; the nightly `jbig2` target fails on the default branch | fuzz on current code green (K-06), structure-aware and metamorphic targets (K-04), timeouts and memory blow-ups counted as failures (K-05) |
| Capability coverage | this file's rows | each row's exit criterion; open and closed counts per tier recorded at each wave's close |
| Surface | 221 C functions; six bindings — Python and JavaScript over the facade, and .NET, Go, Ruby and Java over the C ABI — with Swift as source nobody has compiled, and none projecting the whole facade; eighteen CLI subcommands | the SU rows; `cargo xtask bindings-coverage` (SU-12) |
| Maturity | version 0.0.1, nothing published, one release run watched | SU-17, SU-18 and the owner's release decisions (H-113, H-114) |

## Order of work

**First, in this order**, each because it unblocks the most: CD-19 (the
standard-14 overflow font's code 32 under `word-spacing`: a letter drawn
over the next and read out of order, in books with marks or none — the one
known overprint the October 2026 release carries, which the owner accepted
for that release and put on top, 10 October 2026); MS-11 (CI on
current code) and K-06 (the fuzz failure), with H-01 and H-02; K-01 (the
external graphics state) and K-02 (default colour spaces), because both
change what every page draws; MS-01, MS-04, MS-06 and MS-02 (the speed and
footprint instruments); P-01…P-05 (algorithmic, small, a 3–30× win each on
its operation); then the capability tiers in the order below, each ordered
inside by corpus evidence (ruling 3 as amended), with signatures'
SG-01…SG-09 and form JavaScript's FJ-01…FJ-04 started as soon as their
owner decisions (H-32…H-36) are in; then container depth; OCR last.

**Dependencies that cross tiers.** *Depends on* overrides tier order: a
row waits for every row it names, wherever that row sits, and the named row
is pulled forward to meet it. These pull a later tier's row forward: SG-01
→ P-13; AN-06 → ED-36d; FJ-01 → AN-31; FJ-03e → AN-30; FJ-06 → AN-15; FJ-07
→ AN-24; FJ-11 → AN-11c; SG-06 → AN-17; SG-09 → FJ-17; SG-13 → AN-34;
SE-07 → SG-25; FI-14 → ED-41; FI-27 → ED-33; FO-18 → ED-37; SD-02, SD-12 →
FO-27; SU-12 → RC-18; SU-15 → SG-28; CD-01 → AN-29; CD-04 → FT-34; CD-16 →
FI-33. These are the long chains that run forward across tiers: K-01 →
RC-03, RC-08; RC-01, RC-02, RC-20 → ED-06b → SD-02, SD-12; FO-10 → FO-08b,
SD-02 (part 1), SD-12; FT-12 → SD-02, SD-12; FJ-01 → FJ-02 → FJ-03a…FJ-03f
→ AN-30, CD-12, SU-11; FJ-18 → FJ-10…FJ-13; SG-04 → SG-02; SG-01, SG-02,
SG-05 → SG-06 → SG-07, SG-08, SG-09; SG-06, SG-08 → SG-27 → SG-28; SG-11 →
SG-10 → SG-12; SG-13 →
SG-14, SE-15, AN-34, CD-13; FI-36 → FI-02d, FI-02f, FI-02g, FO-19; FT-30,
FT-31 → SD-07, OC-06, FO-14a, FT-25; ED-01 → ED-02, ED-03; ED-49 → ED-50 →
ED-12, OC-15b, AN-24; ED-26, ED-27 → OC-12; MS-01 lands with every P row;
MS-13 (the corpus runs) closes AN-01, SG-26, SD-01, SD-04, SD-11, FT-30,
FT-31, CD-01 and CD-08.

**The count**: 458 capability, gap and finding rows in the tiers — MS 13,
K 10, P 26, RC 21, FT 43, ED 57, AN 37, FJ 23, SG 28, SE 15, FI 50, FO 42,
SD 26, SU 24, CD 19, OC 24; 14 hard limits (NG-01…NG-14); 107 rows for a
person (H-01…H-133). Every row has one size band, every L and XL row names
its design doc, and no row is larger than XL.

## Tier 0 — measure what is not measured

Each row here is an axis the field judges an engine on and this repository
has no number for, so a claim about it would be the kind of claim ruling 13
exists to prevent. Four rows closed in September 2026, and what closed them
is kept, one paragraph each, because those measurements are the reason the
rest of this file may quote a number at all.

**The production corpus closed on 5–6 September 2026, and paid for itself
at once.** `corpus/corpora.lock` pins a thousand documents a crawler found
on the open web, spanning 462 distinct `/Producer` strings, and its first
run said fourteen of them could not be rendered inside a minute. That was
not the corpus and not the timeout: it was two loops in the rasteriser
indexing with a bounds check per pixel, which cost **9× on a page of a real
word-processor document** and which no fixture corpus had ever said a word
about. Fixing it closed the vectorisation row in the same measurement, with
every fingerprint unchanged on every target ([verification.md](verification.md),
"The rasteriser's row loop"). What that corpus refuses is three files of a
thousand, and not one is a page this engine drew wrongly: one is genuinely
encrypted, and two are HTML pages the crawler saved under a `.pdf` name.

**The JBIG2 counts settled with it.** `MAX_JBIG2_SYMBOLS`,
`MAX_JBIG2_SYMBOL_PIXELS` and `MAX_JBIG2_TEXT_INSTANCES` published the word
"estimate" because the largest `SDNUMEXSYMS` anywhere in the corpus was
**11**, every one of them synthetic, so a cap calibrated on them would have
admitted anything. Over 118 JBIG2-bearing files including the production
corpus's, the largest is **2 478 exported symbols, 2 468 new ones and 4 440
text instances** — real OCR output, from documents scanned by people. The
caps stand at 100 000 symbols and 4 194 304 instances, which is 40 and 944
times what a real document has asked for.

**The symbol-pixel budget settled on 26 September 2026 and brought a
fourth row with it.** `MAX_JBIG2_SYMBOL_PIXELS` is a *pixel* budget and the
figures above are counts, and **neither of a symbol's dimensions is in any
segment header**, so `jbig2_census.rs`, which shares no code with the
decoder on purpose, could count symbols and could not measure one. It
decodes for that half now. The largest dictionary in five corpora spends
**1 568 118** pixels, which the cap clears by 42.8×. The same pass answered
the question the `jbig2` fuzz row had been left on — the largest symbol
*relative to its page*, which over 88 736 corpus symbols is **never more
than one** — and `MAX_JBIG2_SYMBOL_PAGE_MULTIPLE` is the bound chosen from
it, at 4.

**Speed got a ratchet on 19 September 2026, and the ratchet is not enough.**
`bench.yml` has a `workflow_dispatch` on `ubuntu-latest`; fifteen
dispatches of one unchanged revision measured that runner's own swing over
the seven operations — 22.61 % for "extract a page of text", 88.36 % for
"render text at 150 dpi" — and `crates/tinker-pdf/benches/baseline.json`
carries each operation's fastest figure and its own band above its own
swing; `cargo xtask baseline` holds that file, `benches/engine.rs` and the
weekly job's count to each other ([verification.md](verification.md),
"Clocks: outside the suite, and not nowhere"). The old diagnosis of why the
weekly job never worked was half wrong, and the wrong half is the one worth
keeping: **a scheduled workflow runs the default branch**, which is `main`,
hundreds of commits behind `develop`, so the cron runs benchmarked a
months-old tree (H-01). And what the ratchet does not do: `ubuntu-latest`
is a fleet and not a machine — the fifteen runs fall into two groups, four
at 1.00 to 1.16 of the fastest and eleven at 1.30 to 1.36, on one runner
image, and the job records `/proc/cpuinfo`'s model name now so the next
measurement can attribute the split — and a band above that spread is
wide; the text renderer's is 135 %,
and a change that makes it half again slower passes in silence, where the
9× rasteriser regression of 5 September would not have. The swing has not
converged either (57.59 % over five runs, 72.71 % over nine, 88.36 % over
fifteen). The finer comparison is still criterion's own `--baseline`
between two revisions on one machine. **That is why this tier reopens**:
the owner made speed an axis with targets, and the instrument that exists
cannot see a 1.5× regression. The rows below build the instrument tier P is
held to, and the censuses no corpus run has fed.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-01 | **Counted-work guards on every pull request and every target.** A first-party `Work` tally — plain counters, no clock, no `unsafe` — threaded through the operation contexts the work budgets already use, and surfaced as an opt-in statistics value on the facade (`RenderOptions::stats` → `Bitmap::stats()`, the same for text, save, merge and decode) so the guards test the public surface (ruling 11) | Counted-work tests exist one at a time, each written after a quadratic was found (`moving_many_children_is_linear`, `a_hostile_run_of_regional_indicators_is_linear`, `a_line_of_nothing_but_marks_is_linear_and_whole`); no shared counter type; `bounds_ledger.rs` bans `Instant::now` in tests; nothing guards page lookup, per-glyph font resolution, decode counts, bytes inflated or cache hits | `cargo test` on linux, windows, macos and `wasm32-wasip1` runs the guard suite — A1 `page(i)` visits ≤ depth × fan-out nodes and every page of a generated 10 000-page document ≤ 3 × 10 000 in total; A2 work(4 000 pages) ≤ 4.4 × work(1 000) for text, render-all at 36 dpi, rewrite, rotate, merge, split, subset, redaction and validation; A3 font dictionaries resolved per page ≤ distinct `/Font` entries used; A4 glyph masks rasterised ≤ distinct (font, glyph, scale, phase) keys, hit ratio ≥ 98 % on a 5 600-glyph page; A5 inflate calls per stream ≤ 1 per operation; A6 merge deduplication decompresses nothing unless encoded length and encoded hash collide; A7 IDCT output ≤ 4 × drawn pixels for a quarter-scale draw, JPX levels decoded ≤ levels needed; A8 whole-image scratch on the PNG path ≤ 1 × the image; A9 band- and page-parallel renders byte-identical to serial at 2, 3, 4 and 7 threads; A10 allocations O(1) per page. Each guard lands with its P row and is shown failing against the code before the fix, the failing run named in the commit | M | `design/performance.md` (owed; tier 0's speed rows and tier P) | — |

Tier P's targets gate through MS-02's factors, calibrations and relations in hosted CI whether or not this machine exists; it adds the absolute figures as a second, tighter check | S | — | H-110 (the runner), H-109, MS-04 |
| MS-04 | **The benchmark set, replacing the seven** — every operation in tier P's target table, about seventy in thirteen groups: open (23 MB file; every page's size over 3 000 and 10 000 pages; an AES-256 file; a linearized file over a slow source); text (100 pages embedded TrueType; 100 producer-style standard-14 pages; 1 000 and 10 000 small pages; structured JSON; search from scratch and over prebuilt text, plain and whole-word diacritic-insensitive); ## Tier 1 — prove correctness

These come before any new feature, with tier 0. Ruling 13 says the engine
agreeing with itself is the only kind of proof this repository will have,
which raises the bar on what the checks must be: answers computable in
closed form, bitstreams transcribed from the standards' own annexes,
published conformance data, and thousands of documents nobody here
authored. What this tier cannot contain is stated once: the four properties
that left with the oracles ([verification.md](verification.md), "What does
not come back") do not return and are not rows (NG-04).

**What the closed rows of this tier and of the old refusals tier left
behind**, one paragraph each.

**Thirty-six password-refused files became measurements** (5–6 September
2026): [corpus/passwords.tsv](../corpus/passwords.tsv) carries 34 rows, each
quoted to the upstream line that states it, two marked `derived` because no
upstream file names them, and one carrying a SASLprep-normalised form with
the gap that requires it named. Two files stay refused and neither is a
password: one misspells its key length as `/Wength 128`, and one is 91
bytes of a structural tool's own fuzzer output with `/O` and `/U` both
empty.

**`ROTATE_BUDGET` is 10 %**, and the measurement that raised it corrected
the one that prompted it: on pages the construct covers, a page of 11-point
text costs **8.77 %** and stays there as the page grows, so two percent was
out by a factor of four. Ten sits above that and below the tiling class at
22 %; it costs signal — 181 corpus failures become 15 — so the same
measurement is judged again at 3 % as `rotate-tight`, which the ratchet
holds and no run fails on. A page saturated with diagonal edge costs
23.5 %, the defect class's own range, so no budget separates those two
populations.

**Annex F numbering closed on 20 September 2026**, and reading the clause
corrected the row three times: parts 7, 8 and 9 are numbered sequentially
from 1 and the head after them; the head holds the catalogue, the
document-level objects and page one but also part 2 and the primary hint
stream, because the first-page cross-reference section is one subsection
and 7.5.4 makes a subsection a contiguous range; Annex F reserves no object
numbers; and F.3.6 gives the hint stream the last object number in the
file, which the structural tool's suite's `lin1.pdf` shows. The exit
criterion is `crates/tinker-pdf-cos/tests/linearized_numbering.rs` and
`validate/hints.rs`'s `annex_f_numbering`, which find every page of this
writer's output and of 31 linearized files in that suite — `badlin1.pdf`,
its deliberately damaged fixture, named as the one that does not. The flip
found the strict validator's `/E` rule inert on every conforming file; it
recomputes by reachability now, and over the suite's 45 linearized files
none reports `/E`.

**The codec rows closed, and their method is kept because it
generalises.** JPEG arithmetic coding (SOF9 and SOF10) closed on 20
September 2026 and JPX on 23 September, when `BYPASS` and `TERMALL` landed
and Table A.19 left the refusal list — `jpx_attribution.rs` finds the same
39 JPX files, 7 refusals and five reasons after the closure as before it,
none of them a coding capability; every JBIG2-bearing real document in
five corpora decodes but one, the viewer suite's `issue3371.pdf`, a stream
that stops inside a segment, which `Jbig2Refusal::is_malformed` calls damage
and `jbig2_attribution.rs` pins by name. The rule that generalises: *read
the annex list before asserting that a standard publishes no data.* T.88,
T.800 and T.81 each published what this file had assumed did not exist —
T.800 Annex J.10's complete codestream with its packet extents and
intermediate coefficients, B.10.7.1's and B.10.7.2's worked header bit
sequences, T.81 Annex K.4.1's arithmetic-coder vector with its per-event
traces — and four levers came out of the searches: published intermediate
coefficients adjudicate what acts on coefficients (`jpx_annex_h.rs`);
published packet extents adjudicate what moves a packet (`jpx_annex_j.rs`,
`jpx_poc.rs`); B.10.7.2 makes what happens inside codeword segments visible
in the packet header (`code_block_styles.rs`); and a published header bit
sequence is the standard's bytes in and the standard's numbers out. A
specification whose text layer is garbled is still readable: render the
page with a face supplied (`tpdf render --fonts`) and read the image. And
having the specification is necessary and not sufficient — of 182 citations
six implementation specs drew from T.800, 37 were wrong, and a second
reading against the document is what caught them. Two limits are named
rather than hidden: `TERMALL` has no link whose expected output is
T.800's, so Table D.8 is transcribed and asserted as data; and the
arithmetic JPEG statistical model has nothing published to adjudicate it
(NG-07).

**The G4 encoder's exit criterion changed, and the change is the point.**
It asked for the encoder to be held to the T.4/T.6 coder the TIFF tests
carry — a coder this repository wrote, which is the failure ruling 13 and
the self-round-trip rule exist to name. It was held instead to ITU-T T.4
Tables 2, 3a, 3b and 4, read twice, all 195 run-length entries asserted in
`ccitt::tests::the_run_tables_are_itu_t_t_4_s_own`, and to T.88 Annex H.1's
segment 4, 26 bytes of MMR coding a picture the same annex publishes; the
JBIG2 generic-region encoder to the same annex's segment 11. No published
bitstream can pin a context numbering, because a coder's output is blind to
any bijection of it, so T.88's Figures 8 to 11 pin it instead.

**The nine reviewed goldens are this tier's remaining exit, and a person's
reading**: H-60, in the last section. K-08 extends the mechanism to every
new rendered surface.

## Tier P — performance

The owner's instruction is speed verified by tests and an engine optimised
against them. Tier 0 builds the instruments (MS-01 to MS-12); this tier
spends them. **Rules every row keeps**: no `unsafe` in engine crates, so
SIMD comes from autovectorisation-friendly code (fixed-width `chunks_exact`
loops over integer slices, bounds checks hoisted, no calls, libm or `u128`
division in inner loops), from per-build target features (`+simd128` for
wasm), and later from `std::simd` once stable; no third-party crates — the
thread pool is the engine's own on `std::thread`; determinism across
targets — parallel work writes into pre-sized outputs and joins in index
order, and a cache hit returns exactly the bytes a miss would compute;
never panic on untrusted input — every accelerator (a page index, xref
acceleration) validates what a document tells it and falls back to the walk
with a typed warning. **Order**: P-01…P-05 first (algorithmic, small, each
a 3–30× win on its operation), then raster, codecs, parallelism and caches,
crypto and shading, then the rest. Each row lands with its counted-work
guard (MS-01), shown failing against the code before the fix.

**How a target gates, with no person in the loop.** "Today" is this
engine's own measurement of 9 October 2026 on a 4-vCPU 2.1 GHz x86-64
machine, taken outside the tree at `61adcde` — in-process unless marked
CLI, single-threaded unless marked — and MS-04's committed benchmark set
re-derives each figure in-tree before it is quoted as current. Every target
has a form that hosted CI checks and fails on, named in the **Gate** column:

- **a factor** — Today ÷ Target, the speed-up the row owes, rounded down
  to three significant figures so a factor is never stricter than the
  target it stands for — against
  `61adcde`, the *planning baseline*. MS-02's job builds the baseline engine
  and the head in one job on one runner, runs each operation paired and
  interleaved, and fails when the median paired ratio, head over baseline,
  is above 1 ÷ the operation's committed floor. Floors start at 1, so no
  operation may lose time against the baseline; a P row closes in the commit
  that raises its operations' floors to the table's factors with that job
  green, and a later change that gives some of the speed-up back fails the
  same job (only H-78 lowers a floor). The answer does not depend on the
  machine, because both engines ran on it minutes apart. An operation the
  baseline cannot express — a quarter-scale decode, a discarded level — is
  paired with the baseline operation the cell names;
- **calibrated**, for an operation the baseline lacks entirely (a
  capability a later row adds): the target in milliseconds is held as a
  fraction of a calibration operation the same job runs — the baseline's
  "text of 100 dense pages", 751 ms on the planning machine — so a 0.5 ms
  target reads as at most 1/1 502 of it on whatever runner the job lands;
- **relation**: two operations of one build compared in one run, with no
  baseline;
- **held**: a factor of 1 — the operation may not lose time against the
  baseline;
- **counted** or **MS-05**: a counted-work guard (MS-01) or a peak-memory
  band (MS-05), properties of the binary and the input rather than of the
  machine.

The absolute figures are what the pinned runner checks once it exists
(MS-03, H-110), a second and tighter check that never by itself decides
whether a target is met. A target gates because the owner set it —
approved under H-77, and gating as written until the owner re-states it —
never because of the planning-time comparison that suggested the number,
which was run once and is never re-run against a build (H-112).

The deflate sizes are byte counts on the bench's own generated inputs: the
size level 6's parameters produce for each input is recorded once, when
MS-04 commits it, as a planning number (H-112), and the output is held to
1.02 times it exactly. A capability a later row adds brings its row into
this table in the commit that adds it, with a number, never "stated on
first measurement".

Each P row below closes when MS-02's paired job is green with its
operations' floors raised to the Gate column's factors, its relations
holding and its counted guards passing; the bold absolute figures in its
exit criterion are the pinned runner's (MS-03).

## Capability tier — rendering and colour

Parity here means every rendering intent, overprint and separations, every
output colour model and depth, every device-dependent graphics-state key
honoured, every render control a host reaches for, and colour management
complete enough to convert documents (ED-06b) and to write PDF/X (SD-11).

| RC-21 | **A tile byte-equal to the page when an image run's overlap falls outside it** | Ruling 5's guard (`crates/tinker-pdf/tests/render_regions.rs`) holds sixteen fixtures — text, shadings, an image, strokes, groups, soft masks, a tiling lattice and a mesh, turned and cropped — byte-equal tile by tile at 0.5× to 4× and down to a one-pixel lattice, since the tile stopped having a frame of its own (26 September 2026). **One decision is still the canvas's**: whether an image joins the run held back so that abutting images do not conflate is decided by overlap with the fragments the run holds over the canvas only. Two images that overlap outside a tile and abut inside it are one run in the tile and two on the page, and the seam conflates on the page only. Measured 2 October 2026 on three one-sample images over a 100×100 page (the first and third abutting at `x = 40.5`, the third overlapping the second below the tile): the tile over the top half differs from the page on **40 pixels, all of column 40, by 63 levels at 1×**, pinned exactly by `an_image_run_that_overlaps_only_outside_a_tile_is_ruling_5s_named_exception`. This row was deleted on 26 September with the image-run case named in ruling 5 as having no fixture; a review built one, so the row came back, narrowed to the case. Holding the run's coverage over the page frame rather than the canvas reaches it, at the cost of every tile walking every image of a run over the whole page — the trade this row has to make rather than a fix slip in | whether a draw joins a run is decided in the page's frame without a tile paying the page's image work, and that pin becomes a byte-equality assertion | M | — | H-15 (the speed/exactness trade) |

colour — a fallback for every script, every shaping model in use, and text
extraction, search and layout analysis with every option the rows below
list, each held to first-party evidence.

## Capability tier — editing, document operations and creation

Parity here means content edited at the level of page objects and text,
every page operation an office user reaches for, colour and image
conversion, revisions and undo, redaction with no residue, and creation by
API and by layout, each held to the strict validator (K-03).

## Capability tier — annotations, forms and XFA

Parity here means every annotation subtype created, edited, drawn and
exchanged; every field type created and filled interactively; every action
typed; and XFA — removed from ISO 32000-2 but carried by deployed forms,
and read, laid out, scripted and flattened by the field — brought in as
rows. Form JavaScript has its own tier, next.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AN-01 | **Per-subtype annotation payloads** — the measurement | **The payloads landed; the corpus count per family is owed.** `Annotation::payload` is an `AnnotationPayload`, one variant per 12.5.6 family (23, and `None` for an untyped entry), each carrying its table's entries with its table's defaults — `/QuadPoints`, `/InkList`, `/Vertices`, `/L` with `/LE`, `/LL`, `/LLE`, `/LLO`, `/DA`, `/IC`, `/CL`, `/BE`, `/RD`, `/FS` as a file specification, `/Open`, `/State`/`/StateModel`, `/Name` icons, `/Sound`, `/Movie`, `/3DD` and rich media by reference — beside Table 164's and 170's common entries, now including `/C`, `/BS`/`/Border`, `/CA`, `/RC`, `/Subj`, `/CreationDate`, `/IRT`, `/RT`, `/IT`, `/NM` and `/AS`; `carries_required()` says whether a family's required entries are there. Every family is held to a hand-written fixture in `crates/tinker-pdf/src/annotations.rs`. **The cap names what it dropped without mutating anything**: `Page::annotation_list()` returns an `AnnotationList` whose `dropped` counts the `/Annots` entries past 4 096, and a second bound, `MAX_ANNOTATION_BYTES` (64 MiB of copies a page, a `bounds_ledger.rs` row), closes an amplification the listing had from the start — one shared object named by 4 096 entries was copied 4 096 times — and is counted the same way in `incomplete`. What is left is the measurement: `crates/tinker-pdf/tests/annotation_census.rs` counts payloads per family and runs nightly in `corpus.yml` from 26 September 2026, but the fetched corpora were not reachable where it was written, so **no per-family count has been taken** and the census holds no floor on one. The census before this row: 14 996 annotations on 982 files, 14 986 covered, 10 refused across 6 subtype names | the per-family counts from the census's first nightly run, recorded here and held as floors | S | — | MS-13 |

## Capability tier — form JavaScript (complete)

The owner's instruction is a **complete** implementation: a complete
ECMAScript engine in the tree — **the 2025 editions of ECMA-262 and
ECMA-402**, the target until the owner moves it to a later one (H-35) — the
whole PDF JavaScript object model as the format's published JavaScript API
reference describes it and ISO 21757-1 standardises it, the full event model
and calculation order, and a sandbox whose every budget is a ruling-1 ledger
row. **Complete is a count that reaches its end, never a ratchet that stays
green while it rises**: a language milestone closes when every
conformance-suite test for its features passes except a committed exclusion
list whose every entry is an owner ruling (H-35), and an object-model row
closes when every member a committed list names (FJ-18) is implemented and
held to an expectation. The engine is a new leaf crate, `tinker-pdf-script`,
PDF-free (ruling 8; its `ALLOWED` row and [architecture.md](architecture.md)
amendment land with FJ-01); the object model lives in the facade. Document
code stays deny-by-default unless the owner rules otherwise (H-36). Every
member that touches the outside world — URLs, submission, mail, printing,
timers, dialogs, collaboration, SOAP, HTTP and databases — is a typed host
request behind FJ-14's gates and never a refusal by design. While a member
is unbuilt it throws a typed refusal naming itself, never `undefined`, and
FJ-18's coverage check counts it as open work, not as done.

## Capability tier — signatures (private-key signing in the engine)

The owner's instruction puts signing with a private key in the engine. That
reverses what [design/signatures.md](design/signatures.md),
[features/signatures.md](features/signatures.md),
[design/pubsec.md](design/pubsec.md) and `SECURITY.md` stated as a
non-goal — "no key parsing … no RSA/ECDSA signing arithmetic", "a boundary
a future signing feature would have to cross deliberately, not drift
across" — and each of them now points here; the ruling that records the
crossing is the owner's (H-09). `bignum` today skips constant-time
discipline because every value it sees is public; from SG-01 on, every
secret operand takes the constant-time path. The engine still links no
random number generator: entropy is the caller's, and ECDSA nonces are
deterministic by default.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |

RFC 3161 signature timestamps have left — imprint, signature, `TSTInfo` digest, the authority certificate's EKU and ESS binding, and its chain at `genTime`, against a token an outside timestamp authority made once. **What is left is measurement, not work**, none of it taken because the corpora could not be fetched where these landed: `verdicts.rs`'s pin for `bug854315.pdf`'s unattributed signer — its assertion that the signature is no longer `NotChecked` is an expectation nobody has measured, and would fail on an MD5 digest, unusable coverage or a missing certificate; what the verdict makes of the corpus's seven timestamp tokens; and a re-run of `cms_census.rs`, whose pins (27 blobs parsed, 7 nested tokens) predate the strict decoding of RFC 2634's first-version `signingCertificate`, which fails a whole `ContentInfo` on a malformed one as the second version always did, and which RFC 3161 tokens normally carry ([features/signatures.md](features/signatures.md); [design/signatures.md](design/signatures.md)) | the census's first run with these, recorded and pinned | S | — | MS-13 |
| SG-27 | **A PKCS#11 signing provider** for keys held in tokens, smart cards and HSMs: a `RawSigner` for `tpdf sign` and the bindings that loads the PKCS#11 module a caller names, finds a key by label or id, and signs SG-06's to-be-signed attributes — kept outside the engine crates, as FT-13's font provider is, because loading a module needs the platform's loader and `unsafe` | absent — the owner's parity decision of 9 October 2026; SG-06 adds the `RawSigner` seam and nothing implements it for a token (`rg -i 'pkcs#\?11\|cryptoki' crates tools` finds nothing) | CI signs `testdata/` fixtures through a software token that holds a generated key (an outside program hosts the key and executes the operation; it judges nothing) and the engine's own verdict verifies every output; a refused operation, a wrong PIN and a missing key are typed errors; `tpdf sign --pkcs11` runs it; real hardware is H-117's confirmation | M | — | SG-06, SG-08, H-34 |
| SG-28 | **Operating-system key stores as signing providers**: Windows CNG and its certificate store, the macOS keychain, and the Android Keystore through the Android binding — each a `RawSigner` outside the engine crates, the key never leaving the store | absent — the owner's parity decision of 9 October 2026; no platform key store is reached (`rg -i 'keychain\|keystore\|\bcng\b' crates tools bindings` finds nothing) | CI legs on Windows and macOS generate a non-exportable key into the runner's own store, sign `testdata/` fixtures through it, and the engine's verdict verifies the outputs; the Android provider signs in the AAR's emulator test; only a signature value crosses from the store | M | — | SG-27, SU-15, H-34 |

## Capability tier — security

Parity here means every standard security handler and crypt filter read
and written (the legacy ones behind an explicit option), the PDF 2.0
extensions for authenticated encryption and integrity, public-key
encryption in every recipient shape, and the audits a security reviewer
reaches for — hidden information, active content, disarm and
reconstruction — each a typed report before it is a removal.

## Capability tier — formats out and print production

Parity here means every raster and vector output a host asks for, the print
streams a printer takes, the container and office formats a reader takes
away, and the print-production work — flattening, separations, trapping,
preflight — that the standards tier's conversions need.

## Capability tier — standards

Parity and more here means: validate **and convert** to every ISO profile,
check the object grammar against the published model, accessibility from
checking to remediation, PDF 2.0 complete with its extensions, and
e-invoices.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SD-01 | **PDF/A validation, complete**: the 39 staged rules and every machine-checkable clause of parts 1a/1b, 2a/2b/2u, 3a/3b/3u and 4/4e/4f, each finding with clause, test number and object path | **39 staged rules** (39 until October 2026, 38 after its rules landed, 39 again since 6.7.2's wider reading was staged). Agreement with the archival validator's conformance corpus is 1 948 of 2 371 PDF/A files — 830 of 831 `pass`, 1 118 of 1 540 `fail` — **measured before the rules below landed, and not re-measured since**: they were written where the corpus is not reachable, each is held to hand-built fixtures and twins, and the next nightly `pdfa_ledger.rs` run owes the agreement figure, the false-positive count (830 of 831 must hold) and the ledger rows each may have made stale. **Landed since, October 2026:** the encoding-CMap clauses (6.3.3.1 and 6.3.3.3, 6.2.11.3.1 and 6.2.11.3.3, 6.2.10.3.x) — a CMap embedded unless the part admits its name (Identity only under part 1, Table 118 under parts 2 to 4), `/WMode` against the program, no reference off the list, an embedded CMap's collection the CIDFont's — leaving a predefined CMap's collection staged under 6.3.3; the XMP packet header's `bytes` and `encoding` attributes (6.7.5, 6.6.2.1, 6.7.2.1) over the catalog's and every page's packet, leaving other metadata streams' headers staged; part 1's prohibition on a filtered metadata stream (6.7.2), over the catalog's — the scope the archival validator's published profile (`070d39f`) gives the rule, where its older wiki statement (`109b482`) reads every metadata stream; that wider reading is the stricter, unmeasured one and is staged by name, which is why the count went back up by one; the content-stream operator list (6.2.10, 6.2.2) — an operator outside ISO 32000's Table A.1, `PS` included, inside `BX`/`EX` or not, in a page, form or appearance stream — leaving the explicitly-associated-resources half staged; an ICC profile's header — version below 3.0 under part 1 and 5.0 after, device class, data colour space — for the destination profile (6.2.2, 6.2.3) and an `ICCBased` space's (6.2.3.2, 6.2.4.2), leaving the profile past its header staged; and 6.2.4.4's two rules, which removed a staged rule outright — every `/Separation` of one name the same alternate and tint transform, compared as objects with direct against indirect and compression ignored, as the clause itself defines the comparison, and every spot colorant of a `/DeviceN` described in its `/Colorants`; font metrics (6.3.6, 6.2.11.5, 6.2.10.5) for a TrueType or CFF program, each drawn code's `/Widths` or `/W` against the advance of the glyph the engine's own mapping selects (`PageResources::selection`, the renderer's and the subsetter's), leaving a Type 1 program and a glyph reached by guess staged; and part 1's transparency prohibition on an XObject's own `/SMask` (6.4, the archival validator's rule 6.4-2), which was neither running nor staged — only a graphics state's soft mask was judged — and may move the Isartor 6.4 ledger row's count of five. **The unagreed `fail` files fall into families**: conformance level A is validated now — `/MarkInfo`, the structure tree root, every structure type after the `/RoleMap`, and every `/Lang` the object graph carries, which took 19 of the 21 logical-structure fixtures and left `830 of 831` where it was; the predefined schemas' value types are read now — the three RDF containers are told apart rather than collapsed to one array, and a property's text is read against the grammar the XMP specification prints for its declared type (`Integer`, `Real`, `Boolean`, `Date`), which took 167 of the 168 files and left `830 of 831` where it was; what stays staged there is the types no fixture exercises (`Rational`, `URI`, `GPSCoordinate` and the rest), since 467 of the 468 `-fail-` fixtures in those two directories are already reported on; then four on an extension schema's custom value types, and six on the packet header's `bytes` and `encoding` attributes — a running rule now — a /Metadata stream that carries a filter (part 1; running now too), and a packet Isartor calls malformed; then 197 in graphics clauses that run and not far enough — the content-stream operator list, the destination profile's header and the Separation tint transform (all running now) — 97 in font clauses waiting on code-to-glyph mapping, content-stream rules as an interpreter rather than a tokenizer (which is what level A's two remaining logical-structure fixtures wait on, and 6.2.11.7.3's five `/ActualText`-per-character ones with them), recursive validation of embedded files, the output intent's own ICC conformance, and 6.11 and 6.12 with fixtures and no rules. ## Capability tier — surface: CLI, bindings, packaging, API stability, documentation

Parity here means every capability reachable from a shell, from C and from
every binding, packaged where each ecosystem looks for it, with an API that
is checked for breaks and documented by examples that run.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SU-01 | **The CLI's verb coverage**: every capability row whose output a person runs from a shell carries its `tpdf` verb in its own exit criterion; the missing verbs, mapped: `sign`/`verify`/`timestamp`/`ltv` (SG-08), `redact` by area, search and pattern (ED-36a…f, SE-10), `annots` list/export/import/flatten/remove (AN-06, AN-08, AN-13), `forms` list/fill/export/flatten/reset/lock (AN-21, AN-22, AN-13, SG-15), `bookmarks` (ED-17), `labels` and `metadata` get and set (ED-17, SD-23), `attachments` list/add/remove/extract (ED-39), `layers` (ED-40), `convert` (FO-17, ED-42), `create` (ED-51), `optimise` (ED-24), `linearize`, `repair` and `clean` (ED-32, ED-37), `json` (SU-02), `show` (SU-05), `grep` across files (FT-21), `audit` (SU-07), `pages` and `info` inventories (SU-05), `trace` (FO-16), `compare` (ED-22, ED-23), `impose`, `overlay`, `watermark`, `header-footer`, `bates` (ED-08, ED-10, ED-14…16), `recolour` (ED-06a, ED-06b), `trim` (ED-07), `bake` (AN-13), `barcode` (ED-49, ED-50), `js` (FJ-15), `run` (SU-11), `ocr` (OC-11) | eighteen subcommands, ten reading and eight writing (`tools/tpdf/src/main.rs:291-308`). The ten reading ones, `fonts` and `images` among them, and eight that write — `merge`, `split`, `rotate`, `encrypt`, `decrypt`, `attach`, `stamp`, `sanitise` — each a wrapper over the facade (ruling 11): `import_page`, `keep_pages`, `rotate_page`, the encryption on `WriteOptions`, `attach_file`, `import_page_as_form` with `stamp`, and `sanitise`. Three decisions the CLI first made on its own the facade now makes for every surface: a turn that is not a quarter (`rotate_page` refuses it), an empty owner password (the user's, as Algorithm 3 has it for R2 to R4), and `sanitise` with no flag (refused). A writer refuses a flag it would ignore, `--password` on an input that is not encrypted, and a page its list names twice. Every writer saves through `tinker_pdf::write::save`, takes `--font-policy subset\|keep` and prints the pass's report; every writer rewrites, never appends; `split` garbage-collects and `merge` deduplicates; each asks `DocumentEditor::check_save` before it writes and maps the answer into its words (`an_encrypted_later_input_or_stamp_source_is_refused_by_name`). ## Container depth

Debts each container format records about itself, in its feature doc's
refusal table, and the layout engine's reach toward print-grade HTML.

| ID | Item | Today | Exit criterion | Size | Doc | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-01 | **EPUB and HTML CSS: the thirty-seven known and unimplemented properties** — `animation`, `background-attachment`, `border-image`, `caption-side`, `clip`, `clip-path`, `cursor`, `empty-cells`, `filter`, `font`, `font-display`, `font-stretch`, `font-variant-numeric`, `grid` and its seven related properties (`grid-area`, `grid-column`, `grid-row`, `grid-template`, `grid-template-areas`, `grid-template-columns`, `grid-template-rows`), `justify-items`, `justify-self`, `list-style-image`, `mix-blend-mode`, `page`, `resize`, `speak`, `src`, `tab-size`, `text-emphasis`, `text-emphasis-style`, `text-overflow`, `transition`, `unicode-range`, `word-wrap`, `writing-mode` — and the counted remainders (repeating and conic gradients, translucent stops and hints, blurred shadows, 3D transforms, `opacity` as a true group, a second background layer, `reversed()`, bidi overrides and right-to-left tables, flex and multi-column), custom properties and `calc()` | Each is reported as `UnimplementedProperty` with an element count, against the 138 names `IMPLEMENTED_NAMES` carries, longhands, shorthands and aliases alike (`UNSUPPORTED_PROPERTIES` in `crates/tinker-pdf-css/src/property.rs:2424-2462`, counted). The ones that change a paged output: `background-attachment`, `list-style-image`, `writing-mode`, `page`, `clip-path`, `filter`, `mix-blend-mode`, `grid*`; and custom properties, which none of the committed books declares. **What is still counted inside implemented properties**: the language-conditional case mappings (`lt`, `tr`, `az`) and `full-width`/`full-size-kana` against `text-transform`, and `quotes: auto` wherever a quote keyword meets it, since its marks are HTML §15.3.6's per-language table, which is not vendored (CD-14); `reversed()` and `<ol reversed>` against `counter-reset`; `opacity`'s transparency group — §15.1 composites, which differs where content inside the element overlaps its own background, is **owed to the structure writer**, since it needs tagged marked content inside a form XObject, and those elements are counted against `opacity` meanwhile; a second background layer and `background-attachment`; a repeating or conic gradient, a translucent stop, an interpolation hint or colour space, a list past `MAX_CSS_GRADIENT_STOPS` (32), and a gradient whose numbers make a geometry that is not finite (`a_gradient_this_build_does_not_draw_is_counted`, `a_gradient_whose_numbers_are_not_finite_is_counted_and_writes_no_infinity`); a blurred shadow and a shadow list longer than `MAX_CSS_SHADOWS` (32); the 3D transform functions, a `position: fixed` box under a transform (laid out against the page) and a link under one (its active area is untransformed); a `color-scheme` list naming `dark` and not `light`; a `font-feature-settings` alternate index above one, a list past `MAX_CSS_FEATURE_SETTINGS` (32), and `normal` kerning or a feature switched on for text set in the standard 14, which this build draws unshaped without their AFM kerning pairs (`a_feature_asked_of_a_face_this_build_does_not_shape_is_counted`); the bidi overrides — `bidi-override`, `isolate-override`, so every `<bdo>` — and, against `direction`, a right-to-left table, flex container or multi-column container, and, in a right-to-left containing block, an over-constrained block, table, flex container or block-level picture, a relatively positioned box with both `left` and `right`, or an absolutely positioned box placed against one, whose columns, items, margin, inset or side this layout does not turn (`direction_and_unicode_bidi_count_what_this_layout_does_not_do`, `a_block_picture_in_a_right_to_left_block_is_counted_against_direction`); `hyphens: auto` (FT-15), and the hyphen a soft one becomes is U+002D on the page, which extraction's opt-in rejoining infers rather than knows; and `light-dark()`, refused by value on implemented properties. `overflow`'s `scroll` and `auto` print as `hidden`, and a scroll container beside a float is cleared rather than narrowed, both stated in the refusal table. **A defect found and not fixed** ([features/epub.md](features/epub.md)): on a right-to-left item's first line that holds an atomic inline (an `inline-block`, or an inline `<img>`), an `inside` marker is ordered with the run it touches rather than with the whole line box, so it is drawn between the item's first word and the atomic box, and nothing is counted; a fix that moved the marker to the line box's end was withdrawn on review, because it joined the marker to the previous item's text wherever `line-height` is 1 or less. **Left this row since 3 October 2026**, each held by named tests in `epub_paint.rs`, `epub_images.rs`, `epub_shaped.rs` and `epub_reftest.rs` and recorded in [features/epub.md](features/epub.md): `break-before`, `break-after` and `break-inside`; `text-transform` (UCD 17.0.0's full case mappings); `list-style`, `list-style-position`, `counter-reset`, `counter-increment` and `counter-set`; `quotes` with the four quote keywords; `opacity` as a per-fragment alpha; `border-radius` and `outline`; `overflow`, `overflow-x` and `overflow-y`; the `background-*` image family; linear and radial gradients; `box-shadow` and `text-shadow`, hard-edged; two-dimensional `transform` and `transform-origin`; `color-scheme`; `font-kerning` and `font-feature-settings`; `direction` and `unicode-bidi`; and `hyphens` at `none` and `manual`. **The census**: milestone 1's measured 84 distinct names across the fetched corpus's 53 stylesheets and 42 across the committed 8; **the committed figure is re-measured** by `epub_css.rs`'s `the_committed_stylesheets_write_this_many_distinct_property_names` — 42 over the first six books' eight sheets, 44 over all nine books' twelve — and the fetched figure is owed, since the fetched corpus is not in this tree. Against the committed nine, **no unimplemented name reaches an element any more**: `CENSUS.tsv`, re-measured in October 2026's eighth wave, is fourteen rows, every one `light-dark()` refused by value on an implemented property (`background-color` and `color` 19 elements each, `border-top` 16, `border-bottom` 4) | each property's warning stops firing on a reftest pair in `epub_reftest.rs`, and each landing deletes its name from the table; the order inside the row is the fetched corpus's `UnimplementedProperty` counts, highest first — **owed**: that ordering cannot be measured here, so the landings since 3 October 2026 are in the order the row's own list and the committed counts gave; the corpus counts recorded (MS-13) and falling — the design's milestones 2 to 7, written with the replaced box | L | [design/epub-layout.md](design/epub-layout.md) | MS-13 |
| CD-02 | **EPUB layout refusals**: `MaxHeightAsAuto`; an atomic box taller than a page in a table band, a flex line or a column; `ColumnSpanAsNone` below a container's children; a positioned box's static position inside a line (`PositionedInLine`); a column background image; `::first-line`; `content: url()`; `@page` size and margins | Each a typed warning in `tinker-pdf-layout` ([features/epub.md](features/epub.md)). **What is left**, re-read 3 October 2026: `max-height` shorter than its content on a box whose `overflow-y` is `visible`, treated as `auto` (`MaxHeightAsAuto`) — the content past the used height would have to be drawn over the box after it, which a column whose `y` never goes back cannot place, and a straddling child would be painted short; an atomic box taller than a page inside a table band, a flex line or a column, drawn where it starts; `column-span: all` on a box **below** a multi-column container's own children, laid out in its column (`ColumnSpanAsNone`); an absolutely positioned box written inside a line whose inset pair leaves it at its static position, which is the inline formatting context's top left rather than the line it was written on (`PositionedInLine`, a float's own limit: the lines do not exist when it is taken out); a table column's background **image** (`ColumnBoxNotPainted`); `::first-line`, parsed with no box (it needs a second layout pass, CD-15); `content: url()`, generating nothing (a replaced box inside a generated one, and the painter keys a picture by its element, so `::before` and `::after` would share one); and `@page`, skipped — **its size and margins wait on the owner** (H-13): the page box is the caller's `OpenOptions::page` and `PAGE_MARGIN`, and eight of the nine committed books write an `@page` margin — one producer's four books `margin: 10px`, another's three `margin-top: 5pt; margin-bottom: 5pt` (one also `margin: 0pt` on its title page), the fixed-layout comic `margin: 0` — so honouring a book's `@page` over the caller's box changes nearly every page here and is a decision about who owns the page, not a property to implement. **Left this row** in October 2026's eighth wave and on 3 October 2026, each held by a pair in `epub_reftest.rs` with its mismatch reference: a positioned box inside a line taken out of it (CSS 2.2 §9.6); `column-span: all` on a child; `max-height` on a box that clips; `inline-flex`; a block inside an inline (§9.2.1.1's split); a table column's background colour and border; anonymous row generation; `::first-letter`; the quote keywords and `quotes`; `counter()` and `counters()`; `:nth-child(An+B of S)`; and `@supports` | each warning stops firing on a reftest pair in `epub_reftest.rs`; `@page` as H-13 rules | L | [design/epub-layout.md](design/epub-layout.md) | H-13 (`@page`) |

| CD-19 | **A standard-14 letter at the overflow font's code 32 under `word-spacing`** | `paint::OVERFLOW_FIRST` is 32, so the first character a face meets past `WinAnsiEncoding` is drawn at code 32, and 9.3.3 applies `Tw` to every single-byte code 32: under `word-spacing` or `text-align: justify` every glyph after it in its string is drawn a word spacing from where layout measured it, so `مدة معلم.` at `word-spacing: 0.5em` extracts `مدةم علم.`, and where a run ends inside a word the moved glyph is drawn **over** the next run's first: `<p>x <b>αλ</b>φα аб y.</p>` at `word-spacing: 4px` draws its λ and φ both at `60pt` in a default build — present since the overflow font (`epub_fallback.rs`'s `a_standard_14_overflow_letter_at_code_32_takes_word_spacing`; review of bf081ca). On the marks fix's round-4 probe (11 646 synthetic books a build), a default build draws 3 glyphs so on its rows of positive spacing, every one in a book with no mark and drawn so at cd407d5 (which drew 41, and 6 with `bundled-fonts`). A slice that holds a mark draws the glyph after the code 32 as a piece of its own, where layout put it, since a mark ends its string and the glyph after the marked letter is placed by layout too: with the code 32's move, f529a47 drew a letter over another 285 times on those rows (254 with `bundled-fonts`; `مُدَّةَ مُعلِّم.` at `word-spacing: 4px` drew the LAM and the AIN both at `48pt`; `a_standard_14_pointed_arabic_letter_is_not_drawn_over_the_next_under_word_spacing`), and on that probe no book that holds a mark draws one now. A slice with **no** mark is drawn as cd407d5 drew it, in a book that holds marks too, and narrower pointed words can give such a slice the line its mark-free twin has, and that twin's overprint: the round-4 re-review's probe of 528 pointed paragraphs holding a mark-free bold run found 18 books (47 pairs) in a default build and 4 (5 pairs) with `bundled-fonts` that draw a glyph over another where cd407d5 did not, each as its mark-free twin does at cd407d5 (`مُدَّةَ مُعلِّم. <b>مدة معلم.</b>` eight times at `word-spacing: 4px` draws a MEM over the full stop at `270pt`) — accepted by the owner for the October 2026 release, and the reason this row is first in the order of work. Besides the gap before the next word and the glyph after a run that ends inside a word, the moved glyph lands on punctuation another resource draws right after the word (`معلم.`'s MEM on its `WinAnsiEncoding` full stop). Writing the overflow font at `0 Tw`, measured on the marks fix's round-3 probe (9 984 synthetic books a build), conserves 25 more books under word spacing and 8 fewer, 6 of them with no mark: they conserved only because the misplacement closed a word gap past half an em, which otherwise cuts a right-to-left line into pieces ruling 14 reads in drawn order | the overflow font's code 32 drawn at no word spacing (written at `0 Tw`, or code 32 left unused), no glyph drawn over another under `word-spacing` or justification in any book, with or without marks, and no book that conserves today ceasing to — which needs ruling 14 to read a right-to-left line cut at its word gaps as one | S | [features/epub.md](features/epub.md) | — |

## OCR — the last implementation tier

The owner's instruction puts OCR in scope and last, as a later
implementation. Everything here is in-tree: preprocessing, layout analysis,
the recogniser and its models — built and trained here from data with known
licences, or vendored as data with provenance where the owner admits it
(H-39). Accuracy is never adjudicated by another recogniser (ruling 13):
first-party synthetic pages — known text rendered and degraded
deterministically — give a machine-checkable error rate in `cargo test`,
and real scans need ground truth a person transcribed (H-71). The import
seam (OC-01) is not recognition and may run ahead of the tier.

## Named non-goals

**Hard limits only**, each for a reason outside this repository's power,
kept in one place so none is mistaken for an omission. Until 9 October 2026
this section also held decisions this repository took and kept; the owner's
parity decision moved every one of them into the tiers above (the table at
the end of this section says where), because a scope choice is a row,
however late its tier. Each entry below names what would move it.

- **NG-01 — RAR's compression** (methods 1–5, solid archives), **RAR 4**
  entirely, and writing RAR. RAR's compression has no published
  specification and one decoder whose licence bars derivation; RAR 4 is
  written by no producer here, so a decoder would be unadjudicated under
  ruling 13 ([features/cbz.md](features/cbz.md),
  [design/comic-archives.md](design/comic-archives.md)). RAR 5's stored
  entries open; its encryption is SE-14.
- **NG-02 — The legacy Mac OS encoding tables**, and therefore a Macintosh
  `cmap` subtable read in a non-Roman encoding. The vendor's published
  mapping files disclaim warranty and grant no redistribution rights, so
  they have no SPDX identifier `deny.toml` allows and `cargo xtask vendor`
  refuses them — the same limit as NG-03, reached from the other direction.
  It costs nothing measurable: 28 of the corpus's 7 905 embedded faces carry
  such a subtable and **every one of them carries a Unicode subtable beside
  it**, which `glyph_for_char` prefers. What the engine owes and does is
  refuse rather than mis-map — above U+007F a Macintosh subtable returns
  nothing, so a caller can fall back instead of drawing the wrong glyph
  ([features/fonts.md](features/fonts.md)). The licence wall is H-94's.
- **NG-03 — The ICC's own published profile files, bundled.** They carry
  no SPDX identifier, so `cargo xtask vendor` cannot admit them; which
  device an archival document's colours are for stays the caller's
  statement ([design/pdfa.md](design/pdfa.md)). Narrowed on 9 October 2026
  by RC-19, which builds a first-party sRGB profile from IEC 61966-2-1's
  published numbers; the old non-goal, "a bundled sRGB profile", is that
  row.
- **NG-04 — The four properties that left with the oracles**: a reader
  nobody here wrote accepting this engine's output, a second reading of an
  XPS package, a reference CSS implementation, an arbiter for an EPUB
  disagreement. Ruling 13 retires them and [verification.md](verification.md)
  names them. The dated outside comparison a person reads (H-61) is the
  admissible substitute, and it is never a check.
- **NG-05 — JPEG 2000 component precision above 16 bits.** T.800 Table A.11
  allows 1 to 38 — `Ssiz` runs `x000 0000` to `x010 0101`, "component
  sample bit depth = value + 1" — and this build refuses past 16. **The
  coefficient plane is what caps it, not the output sample.** E.1's
  dequantisation clamps a coefficient to `2^(R_b + 2)` sample units, and a
  plane entry is Q12 in an `i32`, so a coefficient on the clamp occupies
  `2^(R + 14)` of the plane's own format: `2^30` at 16 bits, which is
  `PLANE_BOUND` exactly, and `2^31` at 17, which an `i32` does not hold.
  That is not the whole reason and it would be revisitable on its own — an
  `i64` plane would hold it, at twice the 268 MB a 4096 x 4096
  four-component tile already costs, and at the price of re-proving the
  `MAX_PRODUCT` bound the fixed-point 9/7 rests on. **What makes it a limit
  is that there is nowhere to hand the result**: ISO 32000-1 Table 89 gives
  `/BitsPerComponent` as 1, 2, 4, 8 or 16 and nothing wider, so a widened
  sample would be narrowed again one stage later, where the narrowing is
  less visible rather than absent. It is **not the same bargain as JPEG's
  twelve-bit frames**, which decode and are narrowed to eight on the way out
  with `JpegPrecisionNarrowed` recorded: a JPEG coefficient path is fixed at
  the frame's own precision and only the *sample* needed narrowing, while a
  JPEG 2000 coefficient's magnitude grows with `R_b` by E.1, so there is no
  stage at which the wide value does not have to exist. T.800 says the same
  thing twice from its own side: Table A.11's footnote a) warns that "not
  all combinations of coding styles will allow the coding of 38-bit
  samples", and **every profile T.800 names caps the depth at or below
  this** — Table A.45's Profiles 0 and 1 at `7 <= Ssiz_i <= 11` (8-12 bits),
  Tables A.48, A.51 and A.52's Broadcast Contribution and IMF profiles at
  `7 <= Ssiz_i <= 15` (8-16 bits). Zero of the corpus's 39 JPX files reach
  it. `the_coefficient_plane_is_what_caps_precision` in
  `crates/tinker-pdf-filters/src/jpx/tests/bounds.rs` asserts the relation
  rather than the figure, so this entry fails a test if its premise stops
  being true ([features/filters.md](features/filters.md)).
- **NG-06 — JPEG XR's unadjudicated list**: the quantised lossy path past
  QP 1, the first-level overlap filter across a soft tile boundary,
  `HARD_TILING_FLAG`, `SHIFT_BITS`, `TRIM_FLEXBITS`, more than one QP per
  tile, and a damaged codestream's surviving values. All of it **decodes**;
  nothing here checks that the result is *right*, and no amount of testing
  shrinks the list. Ruling 13 bars a second decoder from adjudicating an
  output, so the only thing that could close it is T.832's own conformance
  bitstreams, and those are not freely licensed. It can shrink if that
  changes (H-99) and by nothing this repository can do meanwhile.
  [features/xps.md](features/xps.md) and
  [features/filters.md](features/filters.md) carry the list;
  [design/jpeg-xr.md](design/jpeg-xr.md) carries the reasoning and the
  three first-party evidence legs that stand in an oracle's place. **Not the
  same as JPEG XR encoding**, which was a scope choice and is now FO-20.
- **NG-07 — The arithmetic JPEG statistical model, adjudicated.** F.1.4.4's
  Tables F.4 and F.5, Table G.2, and B.2.4.3's DAC conditioning are read off
  the clause; the coder under them is pinned by T.81 Annex K.4.1, which
  adjudicates Annex D's *coder* outright and says nothing about the model
  that picks its contexts. **T.81 publishes no arithmetic-coded image** to
  adjudicate that with. *The search, so nobody repeats it* (20 September
  2026 unless noted): T.81 at `https://www.w3.org/Graphics/JPEG/itu-t81.pdf`
  (HTTP 200, 1 058 883 bytes, SHA-256 `631031d4…768bf0`), Annex K read
  section by section — K.1 and K.2 quantisation tables, K.3 Huffman tables
  and K.3.3's byte lists, K.4.1 the arithmetic coder vector and K.4's only
  subsection, K.5 to K.10 filters and guidance with no data — and a sweep
  for hexadecimal runs over the whole document finds only those, plus
  `X'FFFF0000'` in D.1; T.83 (ISO/IEC 10918-2), whose data ships on
  diskettes (H-98); T.84 (ISO/IEC 10918-3, HTTP 200, 419 607 bytes, SHA-256
  `cd714dd1…89f6173f`, 84 pages), whose clause 4.2.1 makes compliance data
  available "to parties who wish to determine compliance" and whose Annex G
  specifies the *structure* of the test streams and prints none. What holds
  it meanwhile: the decisions of each model are hand-derived from T.81's own
  figures, and the tests assert **which statistics bin every decision was
  taken against**, not only the coefficients, because a wrong bin decodes
  correctly until the bins have adapted; the search is written into
  `jpeg.rs`'s header. Zero corpus reachability: `jpeg_census.rs` walks 688
  files, 10 606 distinct `/DCTDecode` streams and 10 603 frames, every one
  SOF0, SOF1 or SOF2 at eight bits. What would move it: a real document
  using arithmetic coding, or published data (H-107). What would **not**: an
  arithmetic encoder written here, which would only prove the two halves of
  one reading agree (ruling 13).
- **NG-08 — Transport on `wasm32-unknown-unknown`**: network, file system,
  font directories, process launching. The target has none of them — no
  socket, no file, no process exists to call — and that is the platform's,
  not this repository's, to change; so `local()` there is the host's to
  answer through `FontProvider` ([features/epub.md](features/epub.md)).
  Native transport is not a limit: every capability that needs it is a
  host-seam row that works on every target alike — FT-13, SG-10, SG-27,
  SG-28, FI-27, AN-20, FJ-12.
- **NG-09 — Shaping while reading a PDF.** `TJ` arrays are honoured as
  written ([features/fonts.md](features/fonts.md)): the producer
  positioned every glyph, and re-shaping would draw a different document.
- **NG-10 — TIFF `PhotometricInterpretation` 4 and 32803.** A transparency
  mask "is used to define an irregularly shaped region of another image" and
  is not a picture; a colour-filter array is a raw sensor mosaic, and
  "turning it into a picture is demosaicing, which is an algorithm chosen,
  not a format read, and no data adjudicates one choice over another"
  ([features/filters.md](features/filters.md)).
- **NG-11 — A page's state over time**: SMIL and CSS animation and
  transitions. A PDF page is static; the drawn state is time zero, which is
  what paper shows.
- **NG-12 — Proprietary DRM schemes** (vendor EPUB DRM, licence-server PDF
  handlers). Their keys and specifications are licensed by their owners; a
  host may plug a handler in through SE-08.
- **NG-13 — A machine verdict on PDF/UA's meaning clauses** (ISO 14289-1
  7.1–7.5, 7.7, 7.9; -2 8.2, 8.4). Undecidable by a program by
  construction — "an `/Alt` that lies is a well-formed `/Alt`"; the
  validator abstains by name and a person judges (H-62).
- **NG-14 — The other refusals the tree marks permanent**, which are answers
  rather than gaps: a run whose font is not in scope or whose text matrix is
  not finite cannot be measured for redaction (`features/editing.md:820-821`),
  because with no metrics no glyph can be placed and a position that is not
  a number cannot be compared with a rectangle; the redaction bounds
  `MAX_PLACEMENTS`, `MAX_FORM_COPY_BYTES` and 4 096 `Do`s a stream, each
  named; an XML internal subset and an undeclared entity in EPUB content
  (`features/epub.md:561-562`), because the internal subset is where every
  entity bomb lives, EPUB 3.3 §3.9 forbids one in a content document, and
  XML 1.0's *Entity Declared* constraint governs the other; and `local()`
  font sources (`features/epub.md:600`, NG-08).

**Brought into scope on 9 October 2026.** Before this, six things the docs
called non-goals had already become rows because the reason given was a
scope choice rather than a limit — hinting, a visible signature
appearance, timestamp creation, writing a public-key-encrypted document,
image encoders, and inferred reading order — and four of them closed. The
owner's parity decision moved the rest, each to the rows below; the docs
that stated them now point at those rows.

| Former non-goal or decision | Where it was stated | Row(s) now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| XFA ("removed in ISO 32000-2", filed as a hard limit) | this file's hard limits; `features/forms.md:573` | AN-27…AN-34, AN-31 (FormCalc) — not a limit: the specification is published free and deployed forms carry it, and a reader is expected to read, lay out, script and flatten it |
| Signing keys in the engine; key parsing; signing arithmetic | this file's named non-goals; `design/signatures.md:463-468`; `features/signatures.md:314`; `design/pubsec.md:62-66` | SG-01…SG-09, SG-23, SG-27, SG-28 |
| Revocation fetching | this file's named non-goals; `design/signatures.md:469-481` | SG-10 (parse, evaluate, build requests; the host fetches — NG-08) |
| A bundled root store | this file's named non-goals; `features/signatures.md:315` | SG-14 (trust lists from host bytes); bundling itself → H-27 |
