## Human verification and owner decisions

Every manual verification and every decision that needs a person, in one
section, last in the file. A row above whose only remainder is a person's
act has moved here and left its machine half in its tier, and every row
above whose exit needs a person's act names the H row in its Depends on.
Each row says what the person does, what it blocks, and — where the work can
build toward something meanwhile — the default it builds toward until the
person acts, so the machine work above never stalls on a decision. A
decision or a review also says why no machine can make it; an access,
purchase, secret, machine or release row needs no such column, because each
is a person's for one reason — it takes an account, a payment, a signature,
a legal act or a physical machine. Four groups, ordered by what each
blocks, and the standing duties last.

### Before the next wave

Everything else measures stale code until these are done.

| ID | What the person does | Why no machine can | Blocks | Default until done |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H-01 | **Branch policy**: decide what `main` is — move it to `develop`, make `develop` the default branch, or merge at each wave's close — so the schedules (nightly corpus and fuzz, weekly bench) measure current code | merging to and choosing the default branch are release acts and repository settings | MS-11, MS-13, K-06, every owed corpus figure | add `develop` to `ci.yml`'s push branches (machine); the owner merges `develop` → `main` at each wave's close |
| H-02 | **Watch current code through hosted CI**: open the pull request or dispatch, read the three-OS matrix, wasm determinism, bindings parity (.NET, Go, Ruby, Java), fuzz-target builds; dispatch `fuzz.yml` on `develop` and learn whether the nightly `jbig2` crash is fixed | a person watches runs to completion and owns the merge | MS-11, K-06, every "landed" claim since 26 September | none — waves 6–8 are unobserved on macOS, Windows, .NET and wasm until then |
| H-03 | **Authorise the corpus runs** the replanning session could not make (the owner forbade it to obtain corpora): `corpus.yml` on `develop`, and every census that has never run | the owner's prohibition; a person authorises fetching | MS-13 and through it AN-01, SG-26, SD-01, SD-04, SD-11, FT-30, FT-31, CD-01, CD-08, ED-25's measurement | each owed figure stays "owed" and no row closes on it |
| H-04 | **The footprint budget**: raise the 2.5 MiB gate or cut the default build; per-profile budgets now that scripts, signing, XFA and OCR will each add to it | a product trade between what a browser user downloads and what the default build does | release (the next tag fails the gate), FT-01, FT-02, CD-03, FJ-03f's locale data, OC-08a's models | MS-06 ratchets at the measured size; the release gate stays red as the honest signal |
| H-05 | **Approve this file's scope**: what is in, what is late, what stays refused (the named non-goals) | a product commitment; several rows collide with standing rules (I/O, system fonts, running document code, DRM) | everything above | build in tier order as written |
| H-06 | **The clean-room protocol**: only the planning stage reads other engines' public documentation and source listings, only to inventory capabilities; no implementer reads their code; nothing is copied, paraphrased or ported; no names enter the repository | a legal question for the owner (one compared engine is AGPL) | every capability row | the protocol as stated |
| H-07 | **The reach of the never-name rule beyond this file**: whether it binds every doc or this file only; whether fixture generators named in other docs and provenance count; whether committed fixtures whose producer string names an engine are regenerated | the owner made the rule; for this file it is settled — nothing here names a compared engine, viewer, validator or document tool, corpus labels included — and its reach beyond this file is the owner's to set | K-09's reach beyond this file; four committed fixtures' bytes (`testdata/simple-text.pdf`, `outline-3level.pdf`, `encrypted-aes256.pdf`, `permissions-noprint.pdf`) and their fuzz-corpus copies | this file names no PDF engine, viewer, validator or document tool, each corpus named by its role; provenance files (`corpus/corpora.lock`, `corpus/README.md`, `THIRDPARTY.md`) keep the names, because recording provenance is their job; fixture bytes untouched |
| H-08 | **Ratify the parity decision in the rules**: ruling 3 carries a dated amendment of 9 October 2026 recording the owner's instruction (evidence orders work inside a tier; it no longer decides scope), and `architecture.md`'s locked decisions — the PDF 1.7 baseline with full 2.0 conformance not a current goal, the four-format list, no threading API on the facade — now point at SD-18 and P-15 without being amended | a ruling and a locked decision are amended by the owner, not drifted past | SD-18, P-15, every new format row | ruling 3 as amended; the locked decisions stand, with their pointers, until the owner amends them |
| H-09 | **Ratify the signing reversal**: the owner's instruction of 9 October 2026 puts private-key signing in the engine; `SECURITY.md`, [design/signatures.md](design/signatures.md), [features/signatures.md](features/signatures.md) and [design/pubsec.md](design/pubsec.md) now point their key-material statements at tier SG; a ruling that the engine performs private-key operations — every secret operand on a constant-time path, entropy the caller's, key material zeroised — is the owner's to add before SG-01 lands | "a boundary a future signing feature would have to cross deliberately, not drift across" (`SECURITY.md`) — crossing it is the owner's act | SG-01…SG-09 | the owner's 9 October instruction is the decision; SG-01's design doc drafts the ruling |

### Decisions with a default the work already builds toward

| ID | Decision | Why the owner | Blocks | Default until decided |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H-10 | Whether the save doors refuse what `check_save` names (an API change), and whether the C ABI and bindings follow | `DocumentEditor::check_save` answers whether a save writes an encrypted document's plaintext unasked — a rewrite asking for no encryption, or any unencrypted save of a page or stamp copied in from an encrypted document (`SaveRefusal::WouldDecrypt`; an incremental update is unencrypted when the document holds no key, a key whose `/StmF` or `/StrF` is `/Identity`, or an AES key of a length AES does not take) — or replaces its encryption with only the user's authority over withheld permissions (`SaveRefusal::OwnerAuthorityNeeded`), and `check_decrypt` whether decrypting on purpose is allowed (`tinker-pdf-cos/tests/save_refusals.rs`). Every CLI writer asks before it writes. The save doors do not ask, so the C ABI and the bindings, which save without asking, still rewrite an encrypted input decrypted. `write::save` returns bytes, so a refusal there is an API change, and the docs assign it to the owner | SU-12's `check_save` projection's behaviour; the 0.1.0 freeze (H-114) | project `check_save` and `check_decrypt` everywhere; leave the doors as they are |
| H-11 | Redaction clause (a): accept removal at the use, or require Type 3 procedure streams rewritten | the implementing lane substituted its own exit criterion — removal at the use, with `subset::apply`'s emptying — and called it "the owner's to accept or refuse" | ED-35 | treat as owed work (ED-35) |

### Reviews and manual verification

| ID | What the person does | Why no machine can | Blocks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H-60 | **Read the nine reviewed goldens** against the clauses their headers name; put a name and the date in place of `unreviewed` in each header; empty `UNREVIEWED`. The mechanism is done — `crates/tinker-pdf/tests/render_goldens.rs` parses each `.ppm`'s header, refuses a field that is absent, blank or whitespace, re-renders every family and compares byte for byte, and holds a size ceiling so reviewing one stays a real act; the three injections on the mechanism are each caught by exactly the check written for them — and `UNREVIEWED` lists all nine families because no person has read them. S, and it is a reading rather than work | the tier exists to answer "is this the picture the clause describes", which fixtures sharing the implementer's reading cannot; ideally the reviewer is not the implementer | tier 1's exit |
| H-61 | **A dated visual comparison against outside viewers** on a sample of corpus pages, recorded with tool, version, command and date — an input a person reads, never a check | ruling 13's amendment admits it only as a record | nothing formally; the only route back toward the first lost property |
| H-62 | **Judge PDF/UA's meaning clauses** for documents whose accessibility matters, above all ones this engine writes | "an `/Alt` that lies is a well-formed `/Alt`" (NG-13) | any claim that a written document conforms |
| H-63 | **Walk engine-written tagged documents through screen readers** (two desktop, one mobile): reading order, alternate text, tables, fields, language switches | the output is speech and navigation judged by a user | a credible accessibility claim; SD-09 |

| H-65 | **Judge small text** with stem darkening, hinting and an autohinter | "a fidelity question ruling 13 cannot adjudicate" | FT-04a–d |

### Access, purchases, secrets, machines and releases

| ID | What the person obtains or does | Blocks |
| --- | --- | --- |

| H-98 | Obtain the T.83 compliance data. ITU-T T.83 (ISO/IEC 10918-2) is the compliance data published for exactly the JPEG encoder's purpose, and its own clause 4.4 says the data ships "on 3 diskettes" accompanying the document rather than inside it. The ITU's copy returned HTTP 500 on 15, 16 and 20 September 2026 and the Recommendation's own page says it "is only available through payment"; ISO's returns HTTP 403; the one reachable copy is the standards-preview extract (`https://cdn.standards.iteh.ai/samples/20689/…/ISO-IEC-10918-2-1995.pdf`, 2 642 839 bytes, SHA-256 `6ceaa9ff…b636f358`), which `tpdf info` counts at **15 pages** — the front matter and numbered pages 1 to 11, stopping mid-sentence in clause 5.2.1, where its own contents list puts clause 6's encoder compliance tests on p. 19, Annex B's compliance quantisation tables on p. 28 and Annex C's compressed test data on p. 30. The Internet Archive holds nothing: no full-text hit, a 429 from the availability API and a 404 on a direct Wayback fetch. T.84 (ISO/IEC 10918-3, fetched, 84 pages) says in clause 4.2.1 that compliance data is "available from ISO and ITU to parties who wish to determine compliance". Meanwhile the FDCT is held to A.3.3's equation recomputed in `f64` in the test — the standard's formula, not the standard's numbers — and what *is* adjudicated by published data is Figure A.6's zig-zag read twice, Tables K.1 and K.2 read twice, K.3.3's four DHT byte lists appearing verbatim in the output, and two whole entropy-coded segments derived from Tables K.3's and K.5's printed code words with no implementation in the middle. Fixture hunting that may never end | the JPEG encoder's DCT adjudication (ED-28) |
